are given by

v(W_{m})=\frac{m^{2}}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\begin{cases}0&2\mid m\\
1&2\nmid m\end{cases}\,.

(357)

C.2
\text{I}^{*}_{2k-3}
singularities with
\nu=0
or
\mu=0

For
\text{I}^{*}_{2k-3}
singular fibers with
\nu=0
or
\mu=0
, we are primarily interested in two scenarios: one where the generating section components
(\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{z},\hat{w})
vanish to orders
(0,0,0,0)
and one where they vanish to orders
(2,3,1,4)
. When the section components vanish to orders
(0,0,0,0)
, the associated EDS valuations are
0
, as can be verified using the formulas in
StangeEllTrouble
. The
(2,3,1,4)
situation, however, has nontrivial EDS valuations.

To analyze this situation, we first use the seed model in
Eq.

219
to construct an elliptic curve
E
. Specifically, the
\text{I}^{*}_{2k-3}
singularities occur at
\{v=b_{0,0}=0\}
, so we set
v
and
b_{0,0}
to a prime
p
and set the other parameters to some arbitrary numbers. However, the
\hat{z}
component of the generating section would then be equal to
p
, whereas
\hat{z}
was not proportional to
p
in the other situations we have encountered so far. To account for this, we absorb the
\hat{z}^{4}
and
\hat{z}^{6}
factors in the Weierstrass model into the
f
and
g
of
E
. For example, we can take
E
to be given by the equation

\displaystyle y^{2}

\displaystyle=x^{3}-p^{6}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(3-2p^{k-1}+p^{2k-2}+p^{2k-1}}}\right)x

(358)

\displaystyle-p^{9}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-2+2p^{k-1}-p^{2k-3}+p^{2k-2}+p^{2k-1}}}\right)\,.

This elliptic curve admits the rational point

P\colon(x,y)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-p^{2}(2p-1),-p^{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-3p+p^{k+1}}}\right)}}\right)\,.

(359)

This elliptic curve is non-minimal and has additive reduction modulo
p
. We therefore extend the field by the element
p^{\prime}=p^{1/2}
, let
x\to x^{\prime}{p^{\prime}}^{6},y\to y^{\prime}{p^{\prime}}^{9}
, and divide both sides of the elliptic curve’s equation by
{p^{\prime}}^{18}
. This procedure give us a new elliptic curve
E^{\prime}
taking the form

\displaystyle{y^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle={x^{\prime}}^{3}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-3+2{p^{\prime}}^{2k-2}-{p^{\prime}}^{4k-4}-{p^{\prime}}^{4k-2}}}\right)x

(360)

\displaystyle+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-2{p^{\prime}}^{2k-2}+{p^{\prime}}^{4k-6}-{p^{\prime}}^{4k-4}-{p^{\prime}}^{4k-2}}}\right)\,,

which admits the rational point

P^{\prime}\colon(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1-2{p^{\prime}}^{2}}{{p^{\prime}}^{2}},-\frac{1-3{p^{\prime}}^{2}+{p^{\prime}}^{2k+2}}{{p^{\prime}}^{3}}}}\right)\,.

(361)

The elliptic curve
E^{\prime}
has multiplicative reduction modulo
p^{\prime}
, and hence
E
has potential multiplicative reduction modulo
p
. The rational point
P^{\prime}
, meanwhile, reduces to the identity modulo
p^{\prime}
. Therefore, the EDS valuations should be described by
Eqs.

156
and
157
with
v(f)=6
,
n_{P^{\prime}}=1
and
v_{1}(x^{\prime}_{P^{\prime}})=-2
. Putting everything together, we have

v(W_{m})=\frac{3}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(m^{2}-1}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-m^{2}+S_{m/n_{{P^{\prime}}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p^{\prime},b,v_{1}(p^{\prime}),h,s,w}}\right)}}\right)\,.

(362)

The
S
term should be a constant according to
Appendix

B
. Since the
\hat{z}
component of the generating section vanishes to order 1,

should be set to
3
such that
v(W_{1})=1
. This leaves us with

v(W_{m})=m^{2}\,.

(363)

C.3
\text{IV}^{*}
singularities

For
\text{IV}^{*}
singular fibers, we are primarily interested in the analogous EDS valuations when the generating section is described by
\nu=1,2
or
\mu=1,2
. We first find an elliptic curve
E
by taking the construction in
Eq.

175
, setting
\sigma
and
b_{2,1}
to a prime integer
p
, and replacing the other parameters with numbers such that
f
and
g
are integers. For instance, we can consider an elliptic curve
E
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
of the form

y^{2}=x^{3}+p^{3}x+p^{4}(1-p-p^{2})\,,

(364)

which has additive reduction modulo
p
. After making the same substitutions into the generating section components in
Eq.

176
, we can see that
E
admits a rational point
P
with coordinates

P\colon(x,y)=(p^{2},p^{2})\,.

(365)

We now extend
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by
p^{\prime}=p^{1/3}

x={p^{\prime}}^{4}x^{\prime}
and

, and remove an overall factor of
{p^{\prime}}^{12}
from the expression for

. This procedure gives us a new elliptic curve
E^{\prime}
of the form

{y^{\prime}}^{2}={x^{\prime}}^{3}+p^{\prime}x+(1-{p^{\prime}}^{3}-{p^{\prime}}^{6})\,,

(366)

which admits a rational point
P^{\prime}
with coordinates

P^{\prime}\colon(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=({p^{\prime}}^{2},1)\,.

(367)

The elliptic curve
E^{\prime}
has good reduction modulo
p^{\prime}
, and the original elliptic curve
E
therefore has potential good reduction modulo
p
. In turn,

155
(from Theorem 19 in
StangeEllTrouble
) suggests that the EDS valuations corresponding to

v(W_{m})=\frac{2}{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(m^{2}-1}}\right)+\frac{1}{3}v_{1}(W_{m}^{\prime})\,.

Here,

represents the EDS valuations corresponding to
E^{\prime}
and

with respect to
p^{\prime}
, which should be given by
Eq.

156
. If one calculates multiples of

, one finds that

reduces to the identity point modulo

and that

is
2
. Therefore,

in

is
3
, and the

, can be replaced with
2
. Combining all of this information leads to the formula

v(W_{m})=\frac{2}{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(m^{2}-1}}\right)+\begin{cases}\frac{2}{3}&3\mid m\\
0&3\nmid m\end{cases}=\frac{2}{3}m^{2}-\begin{cases}0&3\mid m\\
\frac{2}{3}&3\nmid m\end{cases}\,.

(369)

C.4
\text{III}^{*}
singularities

C.4.1
\nu=0
or
\mu=0

For
\text{III}^{*}
singularities with either
\nu
or
\mu
equal to 0, we are mostly interested in the EDS valuations corresponding to generating sections whose
(\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{z},\hat{w})
components vanish to orders
(2,3,1,4)
at a particular locus. As an example, we can consider the Weierstrass model in
Eq.

201
and the locus
\{\sigma=b_{0,0}=0\}
. The
\hat{z}
component in this example, which is equivalent to
b_{0,0}
, vanishes to order one at this locus. To account for this fact, we construct the elliptic curve analogue
E
of the fibration given by

y^{2}=x^{3}+fb_{0,0}^{4}+gb_{0,0}^{6}\,,

(370)

where

and

are taken from
Eq.

201
. At the end of the calculation, we will address the fact that these factors of
b_{0,0}
come from
\hat{z}
. As with the other examples, the parameters should be set to appropriate numbers. We set
\sigma
and
b_{0,0}
to a prime
p
and let the other parameters be numbers such that
f
and
g
are integers. For a particular choice of these numbers, we obtain an elliptic curve
E
of the form

y^{2}=x^{3}-p^{7}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(12p^{3}+p^{2}+p-6}}\right)x-p^{11}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(16p^{4}+p^{3}-20p^{2}-12p+11}}\right)\,,

(371)

which admits a rational point

P\colon(x,y)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-p^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2p^{3}+2p^{2}-1}}\right),-p^{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p^{4}-3p^{2}+1}}\right)}}\right)\,.

(372)

The discriminant is proportional to
p^{21}
, and
E
has additive reduction modulo
p
. We then extend the field with the element
p^{\prime}=p^{1/4}
of ramification degree
d=4
, let
x={p^{\prime}}^{14}{x^{\prime}}
and
y={p^{\prime}}^{21}{y^{\prime}}
, and divide both sides of the equation for the elliptic curve by
{p^{\prime}}^{42}
. This procedure leads to a new elliptic curve
E^{\prime}
given by

{y^{\prime}}^{2}={x^{\prime}}^{3}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(6-{p^{\prime}}^{4}-{p^{\prime}}^{8}-12{p^{\prime}}^{12}}}\right){x^{\prime}}-{p^{\prime}}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(11-12{p^{\prime}}^{4}-20{p^{\prime}}^{8}+{p^{\prime}}^{12}+16{p^{\prime}}^{16}}}\right)\,.

(373)

E^{\prime}
has good reduction modulo
p^{\prime}
, and
E
therefore has potential good reduction. Additionally,
E^{\prime}
admits a rational point

P^{\prime}\colon(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({p^{\prime}}^{-6}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-2{p^{\prime}}^{8}-2{p^{\prime}}^{12}}}\right),{p^{\prime}}^{-9}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-1+3{p^{\prime}}^{8}-{p^{\prime}}^{16}}}\right)}}\right)

(374)

that reduces to the identity point on
E^{\prime}
modulo
p^{\prime}
. According to
Eq.

155
, which comes from Theorem 19 of
StangeEllTrouble
, the EDS valuations associated with
(E,P)
should be given by the formula

v(W_{m})=\frac{21}{12}(m^{2}-1)+\frac{1}{4}v_{1}(W_{m}^{\prime})\,,

(375)

where
v_{1}(W_{m}^{\prime})
represents the EDS valuations for
(E^{\prime},P^{\prime})
. Since
P^{\prime}
reduces to the identity point, Theorem 14 of
StangeEllTrouble
states that

\displaystyle v_{1}(W_{m}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{1}(x^{\prime}_{P^{\prime}})}{2}m^{2}+S_{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p^{\prime},p^{\prime},v_{1}(p^{\prime}),0,s,w}}\right)

(376)

\displaystyle=-3m^{2}+S_{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p^{\prime},p^{\prime},v_{1}(p^{\prime}),0,s,w}}\right)\,.

The discussion in
Appendix

B
suggests that the
S
term can be replaced with a constant
s
. If we put these equations together, we find that

v(W_{m})=\frac{21}{12}(m^{2}-1)+\frac{1}{4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-3m^{2}+s_{P}}}\right)=m^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(s-7)\,.

(377)

Now we can address the fact that we included extra factors of
b_{0,0}
in
f
and
g
. Because
\hat{z}
for the generating section actually vanishes to order 1 at the locus in question, we set
s
to 7 such that
v(W_{1})
is 1. In the end, we have

v(W_{m})=m^{2}\,.

(378)

C.4.2
\nu=1
or
\mu=1

For the EDS valuations when the generating section components vanish to orders
(2,2,0,3)
, we can consider the Weierstrass model in
Eq.

254
and section components in
Eq.

255
. We then obtain an elliptic curve
E
and a point
P
by setting
\sigma
and
y_{2}
to a prime
p
and letting the other parameters be integers. As an example, we could take
(E,P)
to be

\displaystyle E:

\displaystyle\,y^{2}=x^{3}+p^{3}x-p^{5},

\displaystyle P:

\displaystyle\,(x,y)=(p^{2},p^{3})\,.

(379)

This elliptic curve has additive reduction modulo
p
. We can obtain a new elliptic curve by extending the field by
p^{\prime}=p^{1/4}
, defining
x=(p^{\prime})^{6}x^{\prime}
and
y=(p^{\prime})^{9}y^{\prime}
, and removing factors of
(p^{\prime})^{18}
:

{y^{\prime}}^{2}={x^{\prime}}^{3}+x^{\prime}-(p^{\prime})^{2}\,.

(380)

This new curve has good reduction, and the original elliptic curve
E
therefore has potential good reduction. The point
P
on
E
corresponds to a point
P^{\prime}
on
E^{\prime}
with coordinates

(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((p^{\prime})^{2},(p^{\prime})^{3}}}\right)

(381)

According to
Eqs.

155
and
156
, the valuations of an EDS corresponding to a point on the original curve follow the formula

v_{p}(W_{m})=\frac{3}{4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(m^{2}-1}}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\begin{cases}S_{m/n_{P^{\prime}}}(p^{\prime},b,v_{p^{\prime}}(p^{\prime}),h,s,w)&n_{P^{\prime}}\mid m\\
0&n_{P^{\prime}}\nmid m\end{cases}\,.

(382)

One can show that
2P^{\prime}
reduces to the identity point modulo
p^{1/4}
, implying that
n_{P^{\prime}}
is
2
. Additionally, the coordinates of
2P^{\prime}
show that
v(W_{2}^{\prime})
is 3. The
S
term, which should essentially be a constant according to
Appendix

B
, should therefore be replaced with 3, leading to the formula

v_{p}(W_{m})=\frac{3}{4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(m^{2}-1}}\right)+\begin{cases}\frac{3}{4}&2\mid m\\
0&2\nmid m\end{cases}=\frac{3}{4}m^{2}-\begin{cases}0&2\mid m\\
\frac{3}{4}&2\nmid m\end{cases}\,.

(383)

C.5
\text{II}^{*}
singularities

For the
\text{II}^{*}
singularity type, we are primarily interested in situations where the generating section components
(\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{z},\hat{w})
vanish to orders
(2,3,1,4)
at a particular locus. As an example, we can consider the Weierstrass model of
Eq.

183
and the locus
\{\sigma=b_{0,0}=0\}
. We must account for the fact that the
\hat{z}
component of the generating section, which is given by
b_{0,0}
, vanishes to order 1 at the
\text{II}^{*}
locus. We therefore construct the
f
and
g
of the
p
-adic elliptic curve by plugging in appropriate values of the parameters into
fb_{0,0}^{4}
and
gb_{0,0}^{6}
from
Eq.

183
; at the end of the calculation, we will account for the fact that these extra factors of
b_{0,0}
actually come from
\hat{z}
. We set both
\sigma
and
b_{0,0}
to a prime integer
p
and set the other parameters to numbers such that
f
and
g
are integers. Performing this procedure with a particular choice of numbers leads to an elliptic curve
E
of the form

\displaystyle y^{2}

\displaystyle=x^{3}-p^{8}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-12p+p^{2}+24p^{3}+12p^{6}}}\right)x

(384)

\displaystyle+p^{11}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-6+p+24p^{2}-p^{3}-26p^{4}+24p^{5}-2p^{6}-48p^{7}-16p^{10}}}\right)\,.

The discriminant
\Delta_{E}
for this elliptic curve is proportional to
p^{22}
. After plugging the same numbers into the
[\hat{x}:\hat{y}:\hat{z}]
section components, we see that
E
admits an integral point
P
with coordinates

P\colon(x,y)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p^{2}(1-2p^{2}-2p^{5}),p^{3}(-1+3p^{2}-p^{4})}}\right)\,.

(385)

The elliptic curve
E
has additive reduction modulo
p
. We therefore extend the field with
p^{\prime}=p^{1/6}
, let
x=x^{\prime}{p^{\prime}}^{22}
and
y=y^{\prime}{p^{\prime}}^{33}
, and divide both sides of the equation for the elliptic curve by
{p^{\prime}}^{66}
. This gives a new elliptic curve
E^{\prime}
given by the equation

\displaystyle{y^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle={x^{\prime}}^{3}-{p^{\prime}}^{4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-12{p^{\prime}}^{6}+{p^{\prime}}^{12}+24{p^{\prime}}^{18}+12{p^{\prime}}^{36}}}\right){x^{\prime}}

(386)

\displaystyle+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-6+{p^{\prime}}^{6}+24{p^{\prime}}^{12}-{p^{\prime}}^{18}-26{p^{\prime}}^{24}+24{p^{\prime}}^{30}-2{p^{\prime}}^{36}-48{p^{\prime}}^{42}-16{p^{\prime}}^{60}}}\right)\,,

which admits a rational point
P^{\prime}
with coordinates

P^{\prime}\colon(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({p^{\prime}}^{-10}(1-2{p^{\prime}}^{12}-2{p^{\prime}}^{30}),{p^{\prime}}^{-15}(-1+3{p^{\prime}}^{12}-{p^{\prime}}^{24})}}\right)\,.

(387)

The elliptic curve
E^{\prime}
has good reduction modulo
p^{\prime}
, and the original elliptic curve
E
has potential good reduction modulo
p
. Since
v(\Delta_{E})
is
22
and
p^{\prime}
has ramification degree
d=6
,
Eq.

155
, which comes from Theorem 19 of
StangeEllTrouble
, states that the EDS valuations associated with
(E,P)
should satisfy the formula

v(W_{m})=\frac{22}{12}(m^{2}-1)+\frac{1}{6}v_{1}(W_{m}^{\prime})\,,

(388)

where
v_{1}(W_{m}^{\prime})
represents the EDS valuations for
E^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime}
. Note that
P^{\prime}
reduces to the identity point on
E^{\prime}
modulo
p^{\prime}
, so according to
Eq.

156
and Theorem 14 of
StangeEllTrouble
,

\displaystyle v_{1}(W_{m}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{1}(x^{\prime}_{P^{\prime}})}{2}m^{2}+S_{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p^{\prime},p^{\prime},v_{1}(p^{\prime}),0,s,w}}\right)

(389)

\displaystyle=-5m^{2}+S_{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p^{\prime},p^{\prime},v_{1}(p^{\prime}),0,s,w}}\right)\,.

According to
Appendix

B
, the
S
term can be replaced with a constant
s_{P}
. Combining everything together, we find that

v(W_{m})=\frac{22}{12}(m^{2}-1)+\frac{1}{6}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-5m^{2}+s_{P}}}\right)=m^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(s_{P}-11}}\right)\,.

(390)

Since the
\hat{z}
component of the generating section vanishes to order 1 at the matter locus,
v(W_{m})
should morally be 1 for our purposes. We therefore set
s_{P}
to 11, giving us

v(W_{m})=m^{2}\,.

(391)
